确认：claim.id 是 PRIMARY KEY（全局唯一），而 claim id 由 extract 生成为 C1..Cn —— **跨会话重复**。第一个会话跑完后，第二个会话写入 C1 就撞 UNIQUE。

这是极其严重的真 Bug：**同一进程里跑第二场就必然失败**。之前所有测试都用 :memory: 库各自独立，所以从没暴露；smoke 也只跑一场。live e2e 第一次跑第一场成功（旧库），第二次跑（新库）第一场就失败 —— 等等，live2 是全新的库，为什么第一场也失败？

哦，不对。第一次运行 live-e2e（bash-1aef3b5d22ef433d）在 live.db 上跑过一次（成功建档 + 跑沙盘，那一次是旧代码，跑出了 9 条主张）。然后我 kill 服务，用 live2/live.db 重启。bash-48a31409c5c242e3 那次跑：建档成功，跑沙盘报 UNIQUE constraint failed —— 这是**全新库**，为什么？

因为 e2e 脚本第一次跑时，第 10 步创建了「自建简历」会话并在其中跑了一场……不，那次是在 live.db。

等等，bash-48a31409c5c242e3 跑的时候是 live2 库，脚本第 3 步建档 → 第 4 步跑沙盘 → 失败 UNIQUE。所以第一场就撞了？

除非... 那次 run 里 extract 抽出的主张 id 重复了？不可能，C1..Cn。

哦！等等。看错误：`UNIQUE constraint failed: claim.id`。也许上一次运行（bash-1aef3b5d22ef433d 在 live.db）——不是这个库。

嗯，但是 48a31409 那次是第一次在 live2 上跑。让我想想 insertClaims：claims 数组里若有重复 id 会撞。extract 里 id 按 ordinal 递增，不会重复。

除非 run 被执行了两次？不。

再看：persistResult 在一个事务里 insertClaims。如果 runSession 期间... 不。

Hmm，实际上 UNIQUE constraint failed 也可能是 claim.id PRIMARY KEY 与 session 里已有的冲突。live2 是新的……

等等 —— 我在 e2e 脚本里加了「修改建议真的产出了」的检查，但那次失败发生在跑沙盘本身。

哦！我知道了。bash-dc135c8de80e4caa 是**第二次**运行 e2e 脚本（在同一个 live2 库上）。第一次运行 bash-48a31409c5c242e3 抛了 TypeError（在读取 view.resumeText 时）——但那是**在跑完沙盘之后**。所以第一次运行已经成功在 live2 库里跑完了一场，写了 C1..C9。

第二次运行（dc135c8d）又新建会话并跑沙盘 → 写 C1..C9 → 撞 UNIQUE。**这就是 bug 复现**。

所以：claim.id 全局唯一，跨会话冲突。这是真 Bug，必须修。

修法选择：
1. claim 表主键改为 (session_id, id) 复合主键，id 列保留为会话内编号。
2. 落库时把 id 加会话前缀。

但 Claim 领域对象里 id 是 C1..Cn，probe.claim_id 引用它，probe.id 是 P1..Pn 也有同样问题（probe 表 id TEXT PRIMARY KEY）。event 表主键是 (session_id, seq) 没问题。llm_call 用 newId 随机，没问题。

最小侵入且正确的做法：**数据库层面加复合主键**，即 claim 表 `PRIMARY KEY (session_id, id)`，probe 表同样。SQLite 支持复合主键。同时 REFERENCES claim(id) 的外键就要改成 (session_id, id)，稍麻烦。SQLite 外键需要引用唯一索引；可以声明 `FOREIGN KEY (session_id, claim_id) REFERENCES claim(session_id, id)`。但注意 `PRAGMA foreign_keys` 是否开启。

替代更简单方案：落库时给 id 加前缀，即 `sessionId + '/' + claim.id`。但那样 probe.claimId 与 claim.id 也要一起改，读回来时 strip 前缀……污染面大，且 ClaimRow.id 会变，前端/报告都用 C1 展示。

其实更好的做法：**保留领域 id 不变，在数据库层用复合主键**。让我看 insertProbe / saveVerdict 等对 claim id 的引用：
- probe 表 claim_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES claim(id)
- saveVerdict(db, claimId, ...) → UPDATE claim SET ... WHERE id = ? —— 这会更新**所有会话**里同 id 的 claim！必须改成 WHERE id = ? AND session_id = ?。

同样 getClaim、setClaimStatus、saveAnswer(probeId)、saveProbeVerdict(probeId) 都只按 id 定位，跨会话会串。

这是系统性缺陷。要么全改成 (session_id, id)，要么落库时改 id。

我倾向于：**在数据库层用复合主键 (session_id, id)**，并让所有按 id 的写操作都带上 session_id。这是正确做法，改动集中在 repo.ts + schema.ts。

但 schema 变更需要 SCHEMA_VERSION 2 + 迁移。目前是 v1，尚无真实用户数据（除 .tmp）。可以直接把 SCHEMA_VERSION 升到 2，并写迁移：v1→v2 需要重建表。或者简单点：既然是未发布项目，直接把 DDL 改成复合主键，SCHEMA_VERSION 升到 2，迁移函数里对 v1 库…… 项目还没发布，data/crossexam.db 是本地开发库。为诚实起见，写一个真正的迁移：v1→v2 重建 claim/probe 表（SQLite 不支持改主键，要 create new + copy + drop + rename）。

不过，等一下。让我先确认另一个更简单的设计问题：probe 表已有 `UNIQUE (claim_id, round, interviewer_id)`，跨会话同样会撞。

好，方案确定：
1. schema.ts: SCHEMA_VERSION = 2；DDL 中 claim 改为 `PRIMARY KEY (session_id, id)`，去掉 id 上的 PRIMARY KEY；probe 同理 `PRIMARY KEY (session_id, id)`，并把 `UNIQUE (claim_id, round, interviewer_id)` 改为 `UNIQUE (session_id, claim_id, round, interviewer_id)`；外键 `REFERENCES claim(id)` 改为 `FOREIGN KEY (session_id, claim_id) REFERENCES claim(session_id, id)`。
2. migrate 增加 v1→v2 迁移。
3. repo.ts: 所有按 id 的写操作带 session_id：
   - getClaim(db, sessionId, claimId)
   - setClaimStatus(db, sessionId, claimId, status)
   - saveVerdict(db, sessionId, claimId, verdict, suggestion)
   - saveAnswer(db, sessionId, probeId, answer)
   - saveProbeVerdict(db, sessionId, probeId, kind)
   - getProbe? 检查是否存在
   - listProbes 已带 sessionId
4. pipeline.ts 调用处同步。
5. 测试同步。

先看看 repo.ts 全文和调用点。另外还要看 db.ts 是否开 foreign_keys。

实际上，还有个更省事的思路：**claim.id 在数据库里存成带 session 前缀的值**，但对外读取时剥掉。不行，污染读侧。

再想想：还有一种做法是给 claim 表加一列 `cid`（会话内编号），主键用 rowid，唯一约束 (session_id, cid)。但列名 id 保留为全局唯一键 = sessionId + ':' + cid。这实际上就是前缀方案。

我选择复合主键方案，干净。

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I need to design the fix. The claim/probe ids are per-session local ids (C1, P1). The cleanest fix: make primary key (session_id, id) and require sessionId in all mutation/lookup functions.

Let me plan:

schema.ts:
- claim: `id TEXT NOT NULL, session_id TEXT NOT NULL, ..., PRIMARY KEY (session_id, id)` and UNIQUE (session_id, ordinal) stays.
- probe: `id TEXT NOT NULL, session_id TEXT NOT NULL, claim_id TEXT NOT NULL, ..., PRIMARY KEY (session_id, id)`, UNIQUE (claim_id, round, interviewer_id) — that's per-claim which is per-session, fine.
- Also probe.claim_id REFERENCES claim(id) — with composite PK, FK must reference (session_id, id). Simplest: drop the FK on claim_id, or add composite FK. SQLite supports composite FK: `FOREIGN KEY (session_id, claim_id) REFERENCES claim(session_id, id) ON DELETE CASCADE`. That works.
- SCHEMA_VERSION 2 + migration from v1: for v1 → v2 we can rebuild tables. But since it's dev-stage with only local data... Better to write a real migration: create new tables, copy, drop, rename. Let me keep it reasonably simple but correct.

Actually the migrate() currently: if current === 0 (fresh), runs DDL then sets user_version. For v1 → v2, need explicit migration steps. Let me implement:

```ts
const MIGRATIONS: Record<number, string[]> = {
  2: [ ...statements to rebuild claim/probe ... ],
};
```

Implementation plan for migrate:
```
if (current === 0) { run DDL; set version; return }
for (let v = current + 1; v <= SCHEMA_VERSION; v++) { run MIGRATIONS[v] }
```
But DDL for fresh must be the v2 DDL. And old v1 DB upgrade: rename old tables, create new via DDL (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS), copy data, drop old.

Careful: DDL contains `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` so running it on an old db would keep old tables. So the v2 migration needs: rename claim→claim_v1, probe→probe_v1, then run the v2 DDL for those two tables, copy, drop old.

Hmm, but DDL includes all tables. Simpler: migration function `migrateV2(db)`:
```
db.exec('ALTER TABLE claim RENAME TO claim_v1');
db.exec('ALTER TABLE probe RENAME TO probe_v1');
// recreate
db.exec(CLAIM_DDL_V2); db.exec(PROBE_DDL_V2);
db.exec('INSERT INTO claim (...) SELECT ... FROM claim_v1');
db.exec('INSERT INTO probe (...) SELECT ... FROM probe_v1');
db.exec('DROP TABLE claim_v1'); db.exec('DROP TABLE probe_v1');
```
But dropping tables with FK references... probe references claim. With foreign_keys ON, dropping is messy. During migration, could `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` — but that can't be changed inside a transaction. migrate does BEGIN. Hmm.

Given this is pre-release and no real user data exists yet (only local .tmp dbs and data/crossexam.db which is gitignored and disposable), I think the honest approach: bump SCHEMA_VERSION to 2, and for existing v1 databases raise a clear error telling the user the local demo DB needs to be deleted? That's harsh but... Actually a cleaner approach that is genuinely correct and simple: since the tables need rebuilding anyway, and v1 DBs only exist in dev, I could write the migration properly with foreign_keys off outside transaction.

Let me do it properly:
```ts
export function migrate(db: Db): number {
  const current = readVersion(db);
  if (current > SCHEMA_VERSION) throw ...
  if (current === SCHEMA_VERSION) return current;
  if (current === 0) {
    db.exec('BEGIN');
    try { for (const s of DDL) db.exec(s); db.exec('PRAGMA user_version = 2'); db.exec('COMMIT'); } catch { ROLLBACK; throw }
    return SCHEMA_VERSION;
  }
  // 升级路径：逐版本执行
  for (let v = current + 1; v <= SCHEMA_VERSION; v++) {
    const step = MIGRATIONS[v];
    if (!step) throw new SchemaVersionError(...);
    step(db);
  }
  return SCHEMA_VERSION;
}
```
And migrateV2:
```ts
function migrateV2(db: Db): void {
  db.exec('PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF');
  db.exec('BEGIN');
  try {
    db.exec('ALTER TABLE claim RENAME TO claim_v1');
    db.exec('ALTER TABLE probe RENAME TO probe_v1');
    for (const s of CLAIM_PROBE_DDL) db.exec(s);
    db.exec('INSERT INTO claim (id, session_id, ...) SELECT id, session_id, ... FROM claim_v1');
    ...
    db.exec('DROP TABLE claim_v1');
    db.exec('DROP TABLE probe_v1');
    db.exec('PRAGMA user_version = 2');
    db.exec('COMMIT');
  } catch (e) { db.exec('ROLLBACK'); throw e; }
  finally { db.exec('PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON'); }
}
```
Note: `PRAGMA foreign_keys` is a no-op inside a transaction, so set it before BEGIN. Also note index names collide: claim_by_session index already exists on claim_v1 (renamed table keeps index). Creating `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS claim_by_session` would skip. So need to drop the old indexes first. Let me drop indexes before rename: `DROP INDEX IF EXISTS claim_by_session; DROP INDEX IF EXISTS probe_by_claim;` Actually renaming a table keeps its indexes attached with the same names. So drop indexes first, then rename.

DDL for claim v2:
```
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS claim (
  session_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES session(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  id TEXT NOT NULL,
  ordinal INTEGER NOT NULL,
  text TEXT NOT NULL,
  category TEXT NOT NULL,
  weight REAL NOT NULL,
  quote TEXT NOT NULL,
  start INTEGER NOT NULL,
  end INTEGER NOT NULL,
  exact INTEGER NOT NULL,
  status TEXT NOT NULL,
  verdict TEXT,
  advice_json TEXT,
  PRIMARY KEY (session_id, id),
  UNIQUE (session_id, ordinal)
)
```
probe v2:
```
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS probe (
  session_id TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES session(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  id TEXT NOT NULL,
  claim_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  interviewer_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  round INTEGER NOT NULL,
  question TEXT NOT NULL,
  rationale TEXT NOT NULL,
  parent_probe_id TEXT,
  quote TEXT NOT NULL,
  start INTEGER NOT NULL,
  end INTEGER NOT NULL,
  exact INTEGER NOT NULL,
  answer_text TEXT,
  answer_mode TEXT,
  verdict TEXT,
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (session_id, id),
  UNIQUE (session_id, claim_id, round, interviewer_id),
  FOREIGN KEY (session_id, claim_id) REFERENCES claim(session_id, id) ON DELETE CASCADE
)
```

Let's check: probe.ts's MAX_PROBES_PER_TURN = 2 per interviewer per turn. If an interviewer returns 2 probes for the SAME claim in the same round, the UNIQUE constraint would reject the second one → crash during persist!

Let me check probe.ts's dedup: `normalizeQuestion` dedupes by question text across the round. But two probes for the same claim in the same round from the same interviewer with different questions — is that allowed? The budget gate `canProbe` checks maxProbesPerInterviewerPerClaim = 2, which allows 2 in the same round. And the prompt says "本轮最多问 N 条" where maxProbes = MAX_PROBES_PER_TURN = 2.

So yes: if the model returns 2 probes both on C1 from tech in round 1, both pass the gates, both get inserted → UNIQUE constraint failed on the second!

That's a real latent bug. It didn't trigger in tests because... let me check. Actually the live run just now produced 12 probes over 3 rounds with 2 interviewers = max 2 per interviewer per round; and probes per claim per interviewer per round... This is a genuine risk — should fix: add per-(claim,round,interviewer) dedup in probe.ts, or relax the constraint. The right fix: within a round, one interviewer asks at most one question per claim. That's also better product behavior (avoid double-asking the same claim in the same round). <details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I need to design the streaming feature. The key gap: orchestrator uses client.complete() everywhere, so live mode has no token-by-token streaming. UX scoring explicitly names 流式 + 思考可视化.

Plan: add streaming to the answer stage (most conversational) — but better: make it generic and add streaming for all five stages, emitting a new RunEvent type `{type:'delta', stage, id?, text}` and `{type:'reasoning', ...}`.

Considerations:
1. Replay mode must stay byte-identical. The replay client's stream() yields chunks from cassette (recorded chunks) or chunkText fallback. If I switch stages to stream(), replay results must remain identical — digest addressing is on the request, unaffected. The text accumulation is the same. So safe.

2. But determinism of events: if I emit delta events, replay produces the same delta sequence as long as chunks are deterministic. Cassettes recorded by scripted-demo have no `chunks` field → chunkText fallback, deterministic. Live-recorded cassettes have chunks. Fine.

3. However, existing test expectations count events (http.test.ts: events=16, from=7 → 9, etc.). Adding delta events will break those counts. I need to be careful and update tests.

Better design decision: make streaming opt-in per run via a flag, so replay path/events unchanged? That adds branching complexity and "two code paths" which contradicts the design philosophy of one code path.

Alternative: emit delta events only when client.mode is live or record? That's mode-dependent behavior — but that's exactly what "streaming" is: replay's fake chunking is already streaming-ish. Hmm, actually replay already yields chunks, so if I stream in replay too, the demo would show token-by-token streaming too — which is *good* for the demo (the video shows streaming). And the event count changes are deterministic.

But wait: the delta events would be persisted to the event table (appendEvent) via onEvent in pipeline. That would bloat the DB with hundreds of rows per session and pollute the "事件 18/21" display and the report. Also SSE `from=` resume.

Design decision: deltas should be ephemeral — streamed over SSE but NOT persisted. So I need a distinction in the event pipeline: `onEvent` persists; add a separate `onDelta` callback for ephemeral deltas. That keeps the event log clean and replay event sequences identical.

Let me design:

In run.ts:
- New type `RunDelta = { type:'delta'; stage: StageName; text: string } | { type:'reasoning'; stage: StageName; text: string }`.
- RunSessionInput gets `onDelta?: (d: RunDelta) => void`.
- Add a helper that runs a stage call: if onDelta provided → use client.stream() and forward deltas, else client.complete(). Hmm, but then two paths again... Actually it's one path with an optional sink; the result text is identical either way (live stream accumulates text same as complete). But replay: `complete` reads cassette response.text; `stream` reads cassette response.text (and chunks). Same text. OK.

Actually simpler and more honest: always use stream(), and forward deltas to onDelta if provided. Then complete() is unused by orchestrator. But then the accounting wrapper `withAccounting` wraps complete/stream — need to check it handles stream. And error semantics: stream() throws LlmError same way.

Risk: switching all stages to stream() means for replay, chunk-by-chunk iteration — fine. For live, SSE streaming — real streaming. And `complete()` remains for other callers/tests.

Hmm, but there's a subtlety: live stream uses `stream: true` in payload; some providers might behave differently with JSON mode. DeepSeek supports streaming with json mode? Usually yes. But risk exists. Safer: stream only the answer stage? No — the most valuable streaming for demo is probe questions and answers.

Let me be pragmatic and lower-risk: introduce a `streaming` capability in orchestrator via a small helper `callStage(client, req, onDelta)`:
- if `input.onDelta` is undefined → `client.complete(req)` (preserves all existing tests exactly)
- else → iterate `client.stream(req)`, forwarding `delta`/`reasoning` events, return the final result.

This gives: tests unchanged (no onDelta) → zero breakage; HTTP layer passes onDelta when SSE → live/replay both stream to browser; persisted events unchanged.

That's the minimal-risk design and honest: "没有订阅者时不必逐片走".

Wait — but pipeline.runAndPersist calls runSession with onEvent. I'd add onDelta passthrough. HTTP app creates the SSE stream and passes onDelta → writes SSE event `delta` / `reasoning` without persisting.

Web: state.ts needs to handle 'delta'/'reasoning' events → accumulate into a live "typing" buffer keyed by stage, display in UI (a streaming panel). That's a bigger web change. Let me scope:

Phase A (server): streaming plumbing + SSE + tests.
Phase B (web): render live deltas (a "实时输出" pane or inline in stage rail), tests.

Let me check pipeline.ts and app.ts to see the wiring points.

Also need: reasoning display — DeepSeek deepseek-chat doesn't emit reasoning_content (that's deepseek-reasoner). But the plumbing should support it. Note reasoningTokens=0 in live run, consistent.

```ts
async function completeWithTokens(client, req, onToken) {
  if (!onToken) return client.complete(req);
  let last = null;
  for await (const ev of client.stream(req)) {
    if (ev.type === 'done') last = ev.result;
    else onToken(ev.type, ev.text);
  }
  if (last === null) throw new LlmError('http-error', '流式响应没有给出结果');
  return last;
}
```

But careful: `withAccounting` wraps stream and records llm_call only on 'done'. Fine.

Another concern: the `answer` stage runs probes **concurrently** (Promise.all). Streaming concurrently means interleaved deltas from multiple probes. That's fine because each delta carries `probeId`.

- `answerOne` uses `client.stream(request)`:
```ts
let text = '';
let reasoning = '';
let result: LlmResult | null = null;
for await (const ev of input.client.stream(request)) {
  if (ev.type === 'delta') { text += ev.text; flush(...) }
  else if (ev.type === 'reasoning') { reasoning += ev.text; flush }

Let me look at how answerProbes handles errors: non-fatal errors go to `errors` per probe. So a stream failure would just mark that probe as unanswered — bad for live UX but not fatal. A fallback improves robustness meaningfully for unknown providers.

I'll implement the fallback in answer.ts: `try stream; catch (err) { if (err is LlmError && (code==='http-error'||code==='bad-request')) fall back to complete }`. Actually simpler and safer: check whether the error happened *after* we already got deltas. If we already streamed text, falling back would duplicate display. So: fallback only if no delta received yet.

OK. Let me write it.

export function partialTextField(raw: string, field = 'text'): string
```
Algorithm: find `"<field>"` followed by optional whitespace, `:`, optional whitespace, `"`. Then read chars until an unescaped `"`. Handle `\\`, `\"`, `\n`, `\t`, `\uXXXX` (incomplete → stop). Return decoded text. If the key isn't found or the value quote hasn't started, return ''.

Let me write it carefully:

```ts
const ESCAPES: Record<string, string> = { '"':'"', '\\':'\\', '/':'/', 'b':'\b', 'f':'\f', 'n':'\n', 'r':'\r', 't':'\t' };

export function partialStringField(raw: string, field: string): string {
  const key = '"' + field + '"';
  let from = 0;
  for (;;) {
    const at = raw.indexOf(key, from);
    if (at < 0) return '';
    let i = at + key.length;
    while (i < raw.length && (raw[i] === ' ' || raw[i] === '\t' || raw[i] === '\n' || raw[i] === '\r')) i++;
    if (raw[i] !== ':') { from = at + key.length; continue; }
    i++;
    while (i < raw.length && whitespace) i++;
    if (raw[i] !== '"') { from = at + key.length; continue; }
    i++;
    let out = '';
    while (i < raw.length) {
      const ch = raw[i];
      if (ch === '"') return out;          // 完整字符串
      if (ch === '\\') {
        const esc = raw[i+1];
        if (esc === undefined) return out;  // 转义还没到
        if (esc === 'u') {
          const hex = raw.slice(i+2, i+6);
          if (hex.length < 4) return out;
          const code = parseInt(hex, 16);
          if (Number.isNaN(code)) return out;
          out += String.fromCharCode(code);
          i += 6;
          continue;
        }
        out += ESCAPES[esc] ?? esc;
        i += 2;
        continue;
      }
      out += ch;
      i += 1;
    }
    return out; // 流还没结束
  }
}
```

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I have the full picture. Let me plan the streaming feature.

Design goals:
1. In live mode, the answer stage should stream deltas so the UI shows text being typed.
2. Add a new SSE event type `answer-delta` (or `delta`) that carries `{ probeId, text }` and is NOT persisted to the event log (it's ephemeral). Wait — but the pipeline's `emit` writes every event to the DB and assigns a seq. If I add delta events, they'd be persisted as events, inflating the event count (breaking http.test's 16-event assertion) and bloat the DB.

Options:
- A: Add delta events to RunEvent, and in pipeline's `emit`, skip persisting delta events but still forward to onEvent (with seq = last seq? SSE id must be numeric string). Hmm, SSE `id` is used for resume. Delta events are ephemeral; giving them a seq would break resume semantics.
- B: Keep delta out of RunEvent entirely; use a separate callback `onDelta` in RunSessionInput. Then pipeline forwards to a separate `onDelta` in PersistRunInput, and app.ts writes SSE with no `id` field. That's cleaner: deltas are not events, they're transient UI signal.

I prefer B, but need to weigh: the frontend's `sandboxReducer` handles `{type:'sse', name, data}`. If the SSE event name is `delta` and has no id, frontend needs to handle `name === 'delta'`.

Hmm, but actually there's a subtlety: `readSseStream` parses blocks; a block without `id:` field just has id=null. Fine.

Also, replay mode: replay client's stream() yields chunkText pieces with no delay — so deltas arrive all at once. That's fine; UI still shows them accumulate. But the offline demo's scripted-demo client's stream() uses chunkText too. But wait — orchestrator currently calls `complete()` for answer. If I change answer to use `stream()`, then in replay mode the cassette entry has no `chunks` (scripted-demo writes response without chunks), so replay falls back to `chunkText(text)`. Good.

But hold on: with pacing (慢放), the frontend event pump throttles SSE events. Deltas would flood the queue with many small events at 220ms each... Actually with pace=220 and say 40 delta events per answer × 10 answers = 400 deltas → 400 × 220ms = 88 seconds of queue. That would make the slow-motion demo drag. Hmm.

Better: throttle deltas in the frontend — coalesce consecutive deltas in the drain loop: when pace > 0, process delta events without waiting? Or simpler: in `pushEvent`, if the event is a delta, merge it into the tail of the queue if the tail is also a delta for the same probe. That keeps the queue small. Actually simplest: coalesce in pushEvent — if last queued event is a delta for the same probeId, append text.

That's clean and keeps the reducer logic trivial.

Now, decision on how deltas flow from LLM to SSE:

**Design:**
1. `RunSessionInput` gets `onDelta?: (delta: AnswerDelta) => void | Promise<void>` where `AnswerDelta = { probeId: string; text: string; kind: 'text' | 'reasoning' }`.
   - Actually reasoning: DeepSeek reasoner gives reasoning_content. Including reasoning would be a bonus for "思考可视化". Let's include `kind: 'text' | 'reasoning'`.
2. `answer.ts`: `answerOne` uses `client.stream(request)` when `input.onDelta` is provided... Hmm, better: always use `stream()` for the answer stage? But then the scripted test client's stream yields `{type:'reasoning', text:'（脚本化思考）'}` and one big delta. That works.

   But careful: switching to `stream()` unconditionally changes behavior for `record` mode — cassettes would then be written by live.ts's `stream()` path with `chunks`, which is fine.

   However there's a subtle issue: replay client's `stream()` reads the cassette, same digest. Fine.

   Risk: existing cassettes in fixtures/llm were recorded via `complete()` — do they have `chunks`? scripted-demo writes no chunks. replay's stream falls back to chunkText. Fine.

   So: always use stream() in answer stage, collect final result from `done` event. Simplest and most consistent — live/record/replay all stream.

   Hmm but one concern: for `complete()` in live.ts, the response is non-streamed and usage comes from `top.usage`. For `stream()`, usage requires `stream_options.include_usage` — already handled. But some providers may not return usage in stream; then usage is EMPTY_USAGE, and the token accounting would show 0. DeepSeek supports include_usage. Acceptable risk; but I could add a fallback: if stream usage is empty, we can't recover. Let me keep it simple and note it.

   Actually wait. There's a bigger consideration: the answer stage is where most calls happen (10 answers). Making all of them streamed is the "思考可视化" win. But the extract/probe/verdict/suggest stages output JSON — streaming JSON is useless for UI (partial JSON is not renderable). So only answer (and maybe reasoning) streams. Good rationale to document.

3. `run.ts`: pass `onDelta` through to `answerProbes`.

9. Tests: server-side test that answer stage streams deltas; web test for reducer delta accumulation + history replay equivalence.

Actually simpler: in `answerOne`:
```ts
let text = '';
let usage = EMPTY_USAGE;
let result: LlmResult | null = null;
for await (const event of input.client.stream(request)) {
  if (event.type === 'delta') {
    text += event.text;
    if (input.onDelta) await input.onDelta({ probeId: probe.id, kind: 'text', text: event.text });
  } else if (event.type === 'reasoning') {
    if (input.onDelta) await input.onDelta({ probeId: probe.id, kind: 'reasoning', text: event.text });
  } else {
    result = event.result;
  }
}
if (result === null) throw new Error('作答没有拿到最终结果');
```
Then parse `result.text`. Use result.text (authoritative full text) rather than accumulated `text`.

Hmm — but concurrency: answerProbes runs Promise.all over probes; deltas from different probes interleave. Frontend keys by probeId, so fine.

    } else queueRef.current.push(event);

});
```

Hmm, but with pace=220ms and merging, each tick shows a chunk of merged text. Good.

Actually careful: with pace > 0, queue holds deltas; when new delta arrives and tail is a delta of same probe, merge. Since each probe's deltas arrive in bursts, this collapses nicely.

Actually, let me think about the design first.

Design decision: I want the answer stage to stream. Key constraints:
1. `answerProbes` runs concurrent Promise.all over probes. Streaming multiple concurrent answers means deltas from multiple probes interleave. Each delta must carry `probeId` so the frontend can attach it.
2. Deltas must NOT be persisted as events (would bloat the event table and break event-count assertions... actually I could persist them, but that's wasteful — thousands of rows). Better: transient events that are pushed to SSE but not appended to the event log.
3. The `onEvent` callback in pipeline gets `(event, seq)`. For transient events I need a seq. Options:
   - Add a separate `onDelta` callback in `RunSessionInput`/`PersistRunInput`.
   - Simpler and cleaner: make the transient channel separate. `onEvent` stays for persisted events; add `onDelta?: (delta: AnswerDelta) => void | Promise<void>`.

Let me go with a separate `onDelta` channel. That keeps the event log semantics untouched (no test breakage on event counts), and SSE writes deltas without an `id:` field so `last-event-id` semantics remain correct (deltas aren't resumable — that's honest: on reconnect you replay events and get the full answer from the `answers` event).

That's a nice design argument for the docs: **delta 不落库、不占 seq、不带 id，因为它不是真相，只是真相抵达的过程**。断线续传靠 answers 事件补齐全文。

Implementation plan:

- `answerOne` uses `input.client.stream(request)`, accumulates deltas, calls onDelta for each `type==='delta'` event, captures `done` result. If no done → throw.

Actually let's keep it simple but valuable: `AnswerDelta { probeId, kind: 'text'|'thinking', text }`. Frontend can show thinking in a muted italic line. That directly serves "思考可视化".

Hmm, but does DeepSeek return reasoning_content for deepseek-chat? No — deepseek-chat doesn't have reasoning. deepseek-reasoner does. So in live mode with deepseek-chat there'd be no reasoning. Still, the plumbing should support it since replay/scripted clients emit reasoning.

Let me include it: `AnswerDelta { probeId: string; kind: 'text' | 'thinking'; text: string }`.

- Note: SSE event name with hyphen is fine.

Wait — but there's a subtlety: `stream.writeSSE` needs to be awaited sequentially; concurrent writes from multiple probes could interleave mid-block. Hono's writeSSE writes a complete block in one call, and Node's stream write is atomic per call for small buffers. Concurrent `await`s on writeSSE from Promise.all — each writeSSE call does a single `write()`. Should be safe enough. But to be safe, serialize writes with a promise chain:

```ts
let chain: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve();
const pushDelta = (delta) => { chain = chain.then(() => stream.writeSSE({...})).catch(() => {}); return chain; };
```
Hmm, that adds latency but guarantees no interleaving. Actually Hono's writeSSE is `this.write(...)` which is a single write. Let me add the serialization anyway for determinism — it's a defensible engineering choice. Actually simpler: keep it simple and just await. Hmm, interleaved writes could produce a corrupted block if two writes happen to be in the same tick... no, each `write()` call enqueues a full string; Node won't interleave inside a single write call. So it's fine.

But there IS a real problem: **delta ordering across probes**. Since each probe streams concurrently, deltas interleave arbitrarily. The frontend keys by probeId, so that's fine.

Another consideration: **throttling**. With live mode, deltas come fast. Writing every token to SSE is fine (that's what streaming is).

**Frontend**:
- `api.ts`: `RunEvent` union — do NOT add delta to RunEvent (it's not a RunEvent, it's a separate transient). Add `export interface AnswerDelta { probeId: string; kind: 'text'|'thinking'; text: string }`.
- `state.ts`: `ProbeView` gains `draft: string` and `thinking: string`. New action `{type:'delta'; delta: AnswerDelta}`. applyEvent's 'answers' case should clear draft/thinking for those probes (since the full answer arrives). stateFromHistory: no deltas in event log → drafts stay empty. Good, no degradation.
  - Careful: `applyEvent` increments eventCount. A delta is not an event — should it increment eventCount? No. So handle delta as a separate action, not through applyEvent.
- `App.tsx`: in `drain`, dispatch sse events. But delta events also go through the same SSE queue. Since `dispatch({type:'sse', name, data})` → sandboxReducer 'sse' case → `asRunEvent(name, data)` returns null for unknown names → returns state unchanged. I should add explicit handling in the reducer: `if (name === 'answer-delta') return applyDelta(state, data)`.
  - Important: with pace (慢放), deltas would be slowed too — that would look terrible (each token 220ms). Hmm. Actually the 慢放 is for replaying the event stream at a human pace. Deltas arriving slowly... Each delta chunk in replay mode is ~24 chars. At 220ms per chunk that's actually a nice typing effect. But if a probe produces 30 chunks that's 6.6s per answer, and they're serialized in the queue... with 10 answers that's 66 seconds. Too slow.
  
  Better: bypass the queue for deltas — apply them immediately (they're not part of the event log anyway, and the pace feature is about the event-level narrative). So in `pushEvent`, if `event.event === 'answer-delta'`, dispatch immediately instead of enqueueing.

New file `D:\CZB\packages\server\src\orchestrator\json-stream.ts`:
```ts
/** 从还没写完的 JSON 文本里增量取出某个字符串字段的当前值 */
export function partialStringField(buffer: string, key: string): string | null
```
Implementation: find `"key"` then colon then `"` then scan forward decoding escapes until unescaped `"` or end of buffer. Return decoded value; if the field isn't found yet return null.

Careful with escapes: `\\`, `\"`, `\n`, `\t`, `\uXXXX`. If a `\u` is incomplete at the buffer end, stop before it.

Let me write it carefully:

```ts
export function partialStringField(buffer: string, key: string): string | null {
  const marker = '"' + key + '"';
  const at = buffer.indexOf(marker);
  if (at < 0) return null;
  let i = at + marker.length;
  // 跳过空白与冒号
  while (i < buffer.length && (buffer[i] === ' ' || buffer[i] === '\t' || buffer[i] === '\n' || buffer[i] === '\r')) i += 1;
  if (i >= buffer.length) return null;
  if (buffer[i] !== ':') return null;
  i += 1;
  while (i < buffer.length && (buffer[i] === ' ' || ...)) i += 1;
  if (i >= buffer.length) return null;
  if (buffer[i] !== '"') return null;  // 值不是字符串
  i += 1;
  let out = '';
  while (i < buffer.length) {
    const ch = buffer[i];
    if (ch === '"') return out;   // 字段结束
    if (ch !== '\\') { out += ch; i += 1; continue; }
    // 转义
    if (i + 1 >= buffer.length) return out;  // 转义序列还没写完
    const esc = buffer[i + 1];
    if (esc === 'u') {
      if (i + 6 > buffer.length) return out;
      const hex = buffer.slice(i + 2, i + 6);
      const code = Number.parseInt(hex, 16);
      if (Number.isNaN(code)) return out;
      out += String.fromCharCode(code);
      i += 6;
      continue;
    }
    const map: Record<string,string> = {'"':'"','\\':'\\','/':'/','b':'\b','f':'\f','n':'\n','r':'\r','t':'\t'};
    const mapped = map[esc];
    if (mapped === undefined) return out;
    out += mapped;
    i += 2;
  }
  return out;  // 缓冲到末尾，字段还没写完
}
```

Hmm, careful: `\uXXXX` surrogate pairs — `String.fromCharCode` on each half gives lone surrogates; concatenating two halves in JS string produces the correct pair actually (since JS strings are UTF-16). Fine.

Then in answer.ts:

```ts
async function streamAnswerText(input, probe, request): Promise<LlmResult> {
  let buffer = '';
  let shown = '';
  let reasoningShown = 0;  // not needed, reasoning forwarded raw
  for await (const event of input.client.stream(request)) {
    if (event.type === 'reasoning') {
      await emitDelta({ probeId: probe.id, kind: 'reasoning', text: event.text });
      continue;
    }
    if (event.type === 'delta') {
      buffer += event.text;
      const current = partialStringField(buffer, 'text');
      if (current !== null && current.length > shown.length) {
        const piece = current.slice(shown.length);
        shown = current;
        await emitDelta({ probeId: probe.id, kind: 'text', text: piece });
      }
      continue;
    }
    return event.result;  // done
  }
  throw new Error('作答的流式输出没有给出结果');
}
```

Mitigation: prefer the **first** occurrence and require it to be a top-level-ish key. Simpler mitigation: search for `"text"` only when preceded by `{` or `,` + optional whitespace? Additional guard: also require that the character immediately before the match (skipping whitespace) is `{` or `,`. Inside a JSON string like `"他说\"text\"是..."`, the char before `"text"` is `\`, so guard rejects it.

Now I have a good picture. Let me design the streaming feature.

Goal: live mode should stream answer tokens to the frontend.

Design decision: Add a `stream` phase to the answer stage. The answer agent produces JSON `{answered, text, citedQuotes}`. Streaming raw JSON deltas to the frontend would look ugly. Hmm. That's an important design consideration: the answer output is JSON. Streaming JSON deltas would show `{"answered":true,"text":"..."}` character by character — not nice.

Options:
1. Stream the raw delta but extract the `text` field progressively on the server side and emit only the human-readable part. That's nicer: server-side partial JSON extraction.
2. Add a separate "thinking" display.

Actually let's think about what's best for the product. The answer draft schema is `{answered: boolean, text: string, citedQuotes: string[]}`. The `text` is the answer prose. Streaming the prose as it's generated would be ideal — but it's embedded in JSON, and the model may put `text` before or after `answered`.

A pragmatic server-side approach: accumulate raw deltas; on each delta, try to extract the value of the `"text"` field using a tolerant partial-JSON scanner; emit only the newly appeared portion of that string. This yields true prose streaming in the UI. That's a genuinely nice piece of engineering and demonstrably "意图控制/工程".

Also worth streaming: reasoning_content if the model provides it (deepseek-reasoner). We could emit a `reasoning` event too. But keep scope tight — let's support both: `{type:'answer-delta', probeId, text}` and `{type:'answer-reasoning', probeId, text}`.

Let's include it since live.ts already yields `reasoning` events; but keep it minimal — maybe skip to reduce surface. Actually the competition explicitly says "思考可视化" (visualizing thinking). We have reasoning events from the LLM layer already plumbed but unused. Supporting it is a real win: `{type:'thinking', probeId, text}`. Frontend shows it collapsed in a dim area.

But careful: pipeline's `emit` appends each event to DB. Delta events at ~24 chars each would explode the event table. So I need a non-persisted channel.

Approach: maintain full raw buffer. Find `"text"` key: search for `"text"` followed by optional whitespace, `:`, whitespace, `"`. Then decode JSON string incrementally: walk characters handling escapes; stop at unescaped `"` or end of buffer. Handle incomplete escape (`\` at end) — stop before it. Return decoded string. Then diff against previously emitted length to get the new part.

Hmm, but "慢放" exists precisely to make replay watchable. Let me think: coalesce deltas in the queue — when pushing a delta, if the last queued item is also a delta for the same probe, append text instead of adding new item. Actually simpler and better: handle in the reducer? No, queue coalescing in pushEvent is clean:

```ts
function pushEvent(event: SseEvent) {
  if (event.event === 'delta') {
    const last = queueRef.current[queueRef.current.length - 1];
    if (last !== undefined && last.event === 'delta' && sameProbe) {
      last.data = {type:'delta', probeId, text: last.text + text}
    }
  }
}
```
